It’s pretty rough—like busted up—and I’ve been staying up trying to deal with leaks or the heat not working right.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I can’t handle stuff, but I’ve been snapping at people more than usual, and then I’m dragging myself into work late. I do construction, and I’ve been messing up things I normally wouldn’t. It’s just… not really like me, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly, but I’ve been pretty wound up since moving into that busted-up room. I’m up dealing with leaks or the heat, and then I’m tired and late to work, messing up stuff I usually handle fine. And I guess I’ve been snapping at people more than I should. I’m not trying to make excuses, but yeah, it’s been getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really sad, I don’t think. Maybe just worn out and frustrated more than anything. I wouldn’t want to say I’m depressed or anything like that if that’s not right. I’ve had some rough moods, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d call it being down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks recently?\n\nPatient: Panic attacks? Um… I don’t think so. Like, I get worked up sometimes, but I haven’t had anything where I felt like I was totally losing control or needed to go to the hospital or anything. Unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean by a panic attack?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any places or situations because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I haven’t been like, refusing to go places or anything. I might put stuff off if I’m tired or not in the mood, but I don’t know that I’m avoiding things because of how I feel. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I guess I still like the same things, I just maybe haven’t been doing as much. But I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy them at all. Like if I get into something, it’s fine. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I still care about the stuff I used to like, I think. Maybe I don’t get around to it as much lately, but it’s not like I hate it now or anything. I don’t know if that counts as less interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not getting as much pleasure out of things as you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I don’t know if I’d say it like that. If I actually do something I like, it can still feel good. I just… maybe don’t get into it as much right away. But I’m not totally numb to stuff or anything, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. My clothes fit about the same. I haven’t been weighing myself or anything, but nothing that I’ve noticed. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really guilty, no. I guess I second-guess myself about stuff sometimes, like whether I’m handling things right, but I don’t think I’m walking around feeling guilty about something specific. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been in a low mood most of the time since you moved into your new room?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say low mood most of the time, exactly. It’s more like since I moved into that busted-up room, I’ve been stressed and irritated. I’ll snap at people, then feel bad about it, and I’m up late dealing with leaks or the heat not working. Then I’m dragging in late to work and messing up stuff I normally handle fine. So… yeah, it’s been getting to me, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Unless I’m misunderstanding what you mean by a panic attack?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any places or situations because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I haven’t been like, refusing to go places or anything. I might put stuff off if I’m tired or not in the mood, but I don’t know that I’m avoiding things because of how I feel. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I guess I still like the same things, I just maybe haven’t been doing as much. But I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy them at all. Like if I get into something, it’s fine. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I still care about the stuff I used to like, I think. Maybe I don’t get around to it as much lately, but it’s not like I hate it now or anything. I don’t know if that counts as less interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not getting as much pleasure out of things as you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I don’t know if I’d say it like that. If I actually do something I like, it can still feel good. I just… maybe don’t get into it as much right away. But I’m not totally numb to stuff or anything, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. My clothes fit about the same. I haven’t been weighing myself or anything, but nothing that I’ve noticed. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been in a low mood most of the time since you moved into your new room?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say low mood most of the time, exactly. It’s more like since I moved into that busted-up room, I’ve been stressed and irritated. I’ll snap at people, then feel bad about it, and I’m up late dealing with leaks or the heat not working. Then I’m dragging in late to work and messing up stuff I normally handle fine. So… yeah, it’s been getting to me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it takes more effort than usual to enjoy the things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little? But I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy them at all. Like if I get into something, it’s fine. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I still care about the stuff I used to like, I think. Maybe I don’t get around to it as much lately, but it’s not like I hate it now or anything. I don’t know if that counts as less interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not getting as much pleasure out of things as you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I don’t know if I’d say it like that. If I actually do something I like, it can still feel good. I just… maybe don’t get into it as much right away. But I’m not totally numb to stuff or anything, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. My clothes fit about the same. I haven’t been weighing myself or anything, but nothing that I’ve noticed. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really guilty, no. I guess I second-guess myself about stuff sometimes, like whether I’m handling things right, but I don’t think I’m walking around feeling guilty about something specific. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been in a low mood most of the time since you moved into your new room?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say low mood most of the time, exactly. It’s more like since I moved into that busted-up room, I’ve been stressed and irritated. I’ll snap at people, then feel bad about it, and I’m up late dealing with leaks or the heat not working. Then I’m dragging in late to work and messing up stuff I normally handle fine. So… yeah, it’s been getting to me, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it takes more effort than usual to enjoy the things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little? Sometimes it takes me a minute to get into it, but once I’m doing it, it’s usually okay. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really lost interest, no. Like, I still care about the same things… I just might not always have the energy to get into them right away. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure.
